A safe point is owed at an allocating call's RETURN; on the non-tracing path nothing allocates. **`_emitted_guard_skips_poll` in `util_gc_census.py` (the coo's reader, CEO-1132's fourth species) fires on a conditional jump above the call landing below the poll and calls it a poll that never runs, without asking whether the CALL runs** — that one omission minted the row, made hq_icon's `8f26f80a0` a real bug (the bignum conviction), and is why their two literal poll_res swaps failed: the baton's own candidate (a) is the mechanism — the entry pushes hold the true result and the pops restore it, so a poll_res at that site protects the hook's leftovers and then discards them. Five more now exist (`PIN_FRONTIER_REG`, `PIN_CAS_TOP_REG`, `PIN_SIGMA_REG`, `PIN_CURSOR_REG`, `PIN_SUBJLEN_REG`), each naming its register, its role and the sites that read it; **proven to fail once** — renaming the subject pin to r12 fails the build at `x86_asm.h:360` with the assert's own message. The gate `test_gate_gc_rbx_is_the_frontier.sh` (wired blocking, adopted into the wiring floor) keeps step 1's claim: every rbx spelling in the templates and the emitter is in a declared set of three, a fourth reds, no template spells the push-rbx saver, and `FAIL_ONCE=1` plants a scratch use and is seen. The emitted output is **byte-identical** (20 Icon benchmark artifacts unchanged, 0 updated), which is the honest claim for a landing that declares rather than changes. ⛔ **THE FLIP CANNOT BE DELIVERED AS THE ROW WRITES IT, AND THE REASON IS A MEASUREMENT: EMITTED CODE NEVER CALLS THE ALLOCATOR.** Zero mentions of `rt_gcheap_alloc`, `rt_ws_alloc`, `rt_heap_alloc`, `rt_pm_struct_alloc` or `rt_agg_alloc` in `src/templates` or `src/emitter`; the safe-point census reads 244 allocating call sites in emitted code and every one is a call into a runtime entry that allocates internally (1655 of 8645 runtime functions reach the allocator); the GOT load the row wants gone lives in `rtx_alloc.s` 7/46/60 and `rtx_str.s` 100, inside the runtime. So no emitted instruction reads or writes the frontier, a cache of it has no consumer, and the arm that reads the emitted `.s` for an advancing rbx presumes a path that does not exist. A cache only pays where the consumer shares the register world: the runtime may hold the frontier in rbx across its own internals because SysV preserves it, but every call from emitted code arrives with the emitted world's rbx and must save and restore it, which costs exactly the memory round trip the cache was to remove. Two designs are with the ceo — an emitted inline fast path for compile-time-constant allocation shapes with the write-back/reload discipline (the real prize, and the allocator's fast path then written twice and held equal by a gate), or defer the flip to its own row with a written design, which is my recommendation because it is a speed win and not a correctness fix while the acceptance path still holds hq_prolog's findall red.

- **CTO-26 (2026-09-12 CDT date-read, cto; MODE NONET, order of work icon,snobol4,prolog) — AN ASSIGNMENT WHOSE TARGET IS A VALUE IS ICON'S RUN-TIME 111: SCRIP `97667e4fd` = origin/main, read back; row `icon-an-augmented-assignment-to-a-comparison-aborts-at-the-binop-guard-instead-of-raising-111-at-run-time` (minted on the coo's fev.icn report, Icon outranking the SNOBOL4 residue) closed by computed `done`.** The IPL compile tier's `gprogs/fev.icn` aborted rc=134 at the BINOP operand-slot guard under the runner's ICONPATH; the node was a BINOP with ZERO operands from `(1 < focuswidth) -:= 1` — `TT_AUGOP`'s fallback for a target that is neither a `TT_VAR` nor an lvalue shape built the BINOP and never pushed its operands (the second BINOP shape after the IR_FAIL fold of `38382dc58`). Oracle: a comparison yields a VALUE, iconx raises 111 "variable expected, offending value: 5" at RUN time with the operation computed, and a failing comparison fails the expression silently. ## THE CTO LOOP
1. Session start as every seat: `fetch` + `ff-only` merge of SCRIP, corpus, .github; `bash SCRIP/scripts/s4e_msg.sh check`; print the banner (`python3 .github/scripts/util_suite_banner.py`).
2. Resume the claimed row (`s4e_msg.sh next` resumes your own claim). One problem at a time; the ceo names the next from the suite table when this one lands (the class blocking the most package programs across lanes).
3. ASM-DIFF-FIRST (RULES.md § Debugging order), the three zetas (ARCH-ENGINE.md), zero comments, LF, `-O0`, no new globals without Lon's banner grant, `make` incremental for the verdict.
4. Push per rung with the ledger line before the next step — the account change is abrupt (ARCH-FLEET-CEO.md § TWO OPERATING MODES, CEO-324); nothing lives only in this session.
5. Land: rebase, re-prove, push code repos then `.github`; FINDING named for the claim; move this LIVE CURSOR; `s4e_msg.sh done <topic>` (computed); telegram `ceo` with the hash.
6. The CTO does not audit, arbitrate, rank, or write law. Escalations go to `ceo/inbox`; Lon's in-chat word wins immediately and is routed into this file the same session.
